        - defect class: names an expression declares for itself alone were missing from the shared binding inventory, so the declaration-name token became an occurrence and uses inside the expression (and, in the relation resolver, bases written inside it) resolved to a same-named outer declaration

        - decision: PHP parentheses are not transparent: a bare name in parentheses is a constant, so `(f)()` calls the constant's value (the review expected `Call`; the code is correct as `Usage`, pinned and documented)
        - decision: not transparent anywhere: dereference `(*f)()`, casts, sequence `(a, f)()`, conditional `(c ? f : g)()`, Python conditional expression; a direct `eval` stays only `eval(…)` because `(eval)(…)` is an indirect eval

### Successful Completion

The implementation of review cycle 1 has completed successfully in 1 h 56 min. During this implementation all 4 review findings were evaluated to see if they could be fixed as a part of this implementation cycle: 4 were fixed, 0 were deferred (see reasons below):

- no finding was deferred
- one sibling check found by the class sweep for 'Cache validation accepts inconsistent reference evidence' was **deferred**: name/ID consistency of containment and declared-relation targets (`parent`, `container`, `members`, `extends`/`implements`) in a stored entry
        - reason: those targets are not built from reference evidence and belong to the type-relations contract, not this specification; the cache robustness matrix deliberately serves a forged `extends` ID under that contract, so changing it needs a type-relations decision
- known limits recorded by the cache finding (not deferred defects): an occurrence or anonymous region deleted from an entry with nothing derived from it, and a `null`-ID `Scoped` occurrence whose name happens to appear elsewhere in the source, cannot be detected without re-parsing

The files changed in this cycle are under `tree-hugger/lib/src/` (`analysis/scope/{mod,rust,ecma}.rs`, `analysis/occurrences.rs`, `analysis/mod.rs`, `cache/stored_evidence.rs`, `dead_code.rs`, `file/mod.rs`, `file/tree_file.rs`, `file/tree_file/relations/lexical.rs`, `file/extract/visibility/ecma.rs`, `queries/provenance.rs`), `tree-hugger/lib/queries/` (C++ `locals.scm`; `references.scm` for Go, C, C++, Perl, JavaScript and TypeScript), the L1 and CLI tests, `tree-hugger/docs/{references,cache-design,type-relations,symbol-kinds-and-visibility}.md` and `.claude/skills/tree-hugger/`.

## Implementation of Review Findings #2

> **started at:** 2026-10-06T15:16:16-07:00

- this implementation is attempting to implement _all_ of the review findings found in 'tree-hugger/features/2026-10-08-scoped-references/review-2.md'
- this is iteration 2 of the review-to-implement cycle
- the worktree already held uncommitted edits to `tree-hugger/lib/src/file/tree_file/relations.rs` and `tree-hugger/lib/tests/l1/symbols_relations.rs` when this cycle started (a concurrent type-relations session); this cycle leaves them untouched
- starting the work on 'Parameter defaults incorrectly see body-local var bindings' at 15:16:58
        - defect class: function-scope binding placement put a function body's `var` in the same scope as its parameters, so a parameter default (evaluated before the body, in an environment without the body's declarations) could resolve to a body-local declaration
        - discovery: `ScopeTree::placement` climbs a `Placement::FunctionScope` binding (`var`, `for (var … in/of)`, destructured `var`, and the direct-`eval` glob) to the function node, which is where parameters and defaults live; `let`/`const`/`class`/body `function` were already in the body `statement_block` scope, so only `var` leaked; the same placement serves the declared-relation tree (`relations/lexical.rs` builds its ECMA bindings through `LanguageRules::binding_sites` and `ScopeTree::placement`), so `function g(x = function () { class D extends B {} }) { var B = 1; }` also kept `B` as text
        - decision: follow ECMAScript exactly: a function whose parameter list holds an expression (an initializer or a computed key in a pattern, `ContainsExpression`) gets a separate var environment, so its body's `var`s go to the body block (`ecma::var_body`, precomputed per scope in `ScopeTree::var_bodies`); without such an expression a `var` named like a parameter redeclares the parameter, and the existing behavior (`NameOnly` over both declarations) is kept
        - decision: a direct `eval` in the body now limits only the body (its `var`s join the body's environment); one inside a default still limits the whole function (conservative)
        - completed: `lib/src/analysis/scope/{mod,ecma}.rs` (`LanguageRules::var_body`, `ecma::var_body`, `ScopeTree::var_bodies`, `placement`), module and `Placement` docs

        - defect class: identifier spelling substituted for binding identity in patterns, so a capitalized pattern name named like the enclosing function never bound (inventing a proven self-call) and a lowercase constant read by a pattern became an invented local
        - discovery: the Rust pattern walker skipped every capitalized identifier in a refutable pattern and bound every other one; the capture fallback in `bind_all` then bound any locals-query record of a pattern name (`Some(F)`) by kind, which masked the first defect for tuple-struct captures and inverted it for a constant (`Some(C)` with `const C` became a local); the walker also descended into generic paths (`None::<u8>`), `const { … }` blocks and closures written in patterns; struct field shorthands were bindings but never reference captures, so a shorthand that reads a constant could not become an occurrence
        - discovery: rustc (`try_resolve_as_non_binding`) reads a bare identifier pattern as a reference when value-namespace lookup reaches a `const`, unit struct/variant or `const` parameter, and as a fresh binding when it reaches a function, a local or nothing; `mut`/`ref`/`@` always bind; this applies to every pattern position, irrefutable ones included (`struct S; let S = S;` and `fn g(S: S)` read the struct; `S { f }` with `const f` is a refutable pattern, rustc E0005)
        - decision: bare pattern names are `BindingSite::bare_pattern` sites (with their `Refutability`) decided in `bind_all` after every other binding is placed, in walk order, by `bare_pattern_meaning` (VALUE lookup at the name; `BindingRule::pattern_constant` marks `const`, unit struct and `const` generic bindings); a read is an occurrence resolved by lookup (`Usage`, `Scoped` to the constant); a capture-only record of the name binds only when the name does
        - decision: unknown identity (an import or glob `use` reached before any binding) is `BindingTarget::Unknown` in a refutable pattern: the pattern token is a `NameOnly`/`Unresolved` usage and every use reaching that binding is uncertain with indirect dispatch, so no proven call and no recursion `Yes`; in an irrefutable pattern it binds, because only a unit struct or single-valued constant could be matched there (documented limit); `None` (the prelude's only unit value) and `Self` never bind; block-level macros already limit their region (`unexpanded_macro`) and are not read as possible constant sources
        - decision: the declared-relation tree keeps placing bare Rust pattern names as value bindings without deciding them: Rust relation lookups read only the type group (`final_categories`/`qualifier_categories`), so the decision cannot change a relation

The implementation of review cycle 2 has completed successfully in 1 h 21 min. During this implementation all 4 review findings were evaluated to see if they could be fixed as a part of this implementation cycle: 4 were fixed, 0 were deferred (see reasons below):

- no finding was deferred
- sibling sites fixed beyond the review's tables:
        - parameter environments: declared-relation lookup (same placement code), body `eval` limiting only the body
        - Rust patterns: generic paths, `const` blocks and closures inside patterns; struct-field shorthands now captured; Scala `_x` and upper-case `@` bindings; `Variable` records no longer emitted for pattern names that bind nothing (Rust pattern paths, constant reads, TS/JS default operands)
        - wrapped callees: direct-`eval` detection through wrappers (`(eval)(…)`), shared with relation scopes
        - cache: `referenced_by` retention, bodiless callables owning a region, Lua empty/comment-only bodies now owning a region (builder and validator share the `body_span` fact)
- carried-over deferral (from cycle 1, unchanged): containment and declared-relation target IDs (`parent`, `container`, `members`, `extends`/`implements`) are not validated for retention; they are type-relations facts, not reference evidence
- known limits documented in this cycle (not deferred defects): a Rust import or glob reached by an irrefutable pattern name is treated as a binding; nested `mut`/`ref`/`@` bindings and bare `match`-arm names bind but have no record from the vendored locals query; TypeScript `(f<u8>)()` remains neither a call nor complete coverage (invocation and coverage agree)
- `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` 40 → 41 (one bump for the cycle; revision 40 was the concurrent type-relations session's)

        - defect class: establishing which declaration a callee name binds was treated as proving that invoking it calls a callable declaration, so a call of an established or candidate value (a constant or static holding a function pointer) received complete coverage and a false `No` recursion verdict

        - discovery: `visit_rust` accepts any `identifier`/`scoped_identifier` callee as direct on purpose; the value limit belongs to the occurrence layer (`Resolver::occurrence` limits the region when a `Call` resolution is `indirect`), so fixing the resolution repairs coverage, summaries-adjacent semantics, the cache and the CLI together

        - decision: the unique member stays `Scoped` to the value's ID with `indirect = holds_a_value(kind)`; every uncertain resolution goes through `Resolver::uncertain(candidates, indirect)`, which ORs in "any candidate holds a value", so `S::<u8>::C()` and trait `Self::C()`/`S::C()` keep their candidate-only binding and are limited; the initializer is never followed and no reference to `f` is created

- starting the work on 'Cache validation accepts false source facts and misordered or duplicate lists' at 17:28:40
        - defect class: stored reference evidence was checked for types, identities and membership, but not for the source-derived geometry and written paths it repeats, nor for the order and uniqueness of candidate and summary lists, so a well-typed entry with a callable region outside its body, an invented, empty or lost written path, reversed candidates, or repeated or reordered summaries was served
        - discovery: `written_path` is always `source[qualifier start..name end]` and the qualifier ends at or before the name's start, so a non-null path is exactly the source text ending at the occurrence's end byte and strictly longer than the name; that check needs no parse and is the same in all 16 languages
        - discovery: a null path cannot be checked against the text: `(identifier) @reference` captures names inside Rust macro token trees (`println!("{}", std::f32::MAX)` has `::` before `MAX` and no path), C++ `::x` and Rust `::std` are rooted with no qualifier field, JS spread `...x` and Perl `->` precede unqualified names. The resolver, however, never reaches a `SymbolScope::Member` record through lookup (members are skipped when `bind_all` binds records, binding sites are parameters/patterns, unbound candidates are `item_records`), only through `is_member` receivers and `resolve_rust_path` paths, both always qualified
        - decision (S09): in Rust/TS/JS, a `null` path on an occurrence whose target or any candidate is a retained Member record is refused ("an occurrence of a member that lost its written qualifier"); evidence: zero violations over 67,100 TS/JS occurrences (250 files sampled from local `node_modules`) and 400 Rust files of this repository, and all 812 real files (Rust, TS/JS and the 16-language fixtures plus `multi/`) are warm `Hit`s under the new reader. Limit, documented: a lost qualifier is undetectable in the 13 languages without binding rules, for a qualified name reaching no member, and for a member the entry omits
        - decision (region): a retained callable's own region must contain its `body_span` (never compared with `declaration_span`); the existing exactly-when-it-has-a-body rule keeps its message
        - decision (candidates): retained candidates must be sorted by `name_only`'s key (name-span start or 0, then ID); omitted candidates are not compared (positions unknown), documented
        - decision (summaries): the bind pass's derivation cannot be rerun because `bound_imports` is not stored, so `summaries_in_first_occurrence_order` checks `RefSet` uniqueness (ID, else name + qualified name) and first-occurrence order: exact positions for IDs (owner's first `Scoped` occurrence of the target, per owner for `referenced_by`), and for an import any of the owner's no-ID `Scoped` occurrences of its name, chosen greedily earliest-first (sound: it never rejects a derived list, e.g. a loop binding `a` before `helper(); a();`)
        - decision: no `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` bump (validator only, stored shape unchanged; 42 reused); no `cache_upgrade` table change (revision-41 entries are already refused by key); goldens unchanged

### Completion Report

**Outcome: partial.** Elapsed: 1 h 28 min (16:56:27 → 18:24:41).

Parent findings: total 4 = fixed 3 + already fixed 0 + blocked 0 + deferred 1 + unresolved 0.

- **R3-F1** fixed: TS `instantiation_expression` is now a generic operand in the shared `generic_operand` table, so call classification, coverage, `new`, direct-eval detection and wrapped member collection all see through it at any wrapper depth
- **R3-F2** fixed: calling a value binding (const, static, field, variable, parameter or unknown kind) is indirect on both the unique-member and candidate paths. The call stays bound to the value; coverage is `indirect_dispatch` and recursion `Unknown`
- **R3-F4** fixed: one shared `ecma::is_direct_eval` predicate excludes optional and tagged calls. Binding placement, the relation tree's base lookup and coverage all use it
- **R3-F3** deferred (every review-enumerated child fixed):
        - fixed: region containment of `body_span`, written-path source checks (invented and empty paths, a `null` path on a retained member), candidate order, and summary dedup/order. All 16 languages, the S01–S03 matrix in Rust/TS/JS, and new robustness-matrix columns are covered by tests
        - open: **R3-F3.S09r**. A `null` written path forged onto a qualified target that is not a retained member is still served. This covers unresolved paths, item paths, omitted members and the 13 languages without rules
        - why it is open: no parse-free check is exact. A text check would refuse genuine entries (Rust macro token trees, leading `::`, JS spread), and any stored marker can be forged the same way as `null`
        - next action: the reviewer or author decides whether to accept this as an indistinguishable-forgery limit, the same kind as the already-accepted deletion of a whole anonymous region, or to request a stored-shape or parse-on-read change

### Completion Report

**Outcome: complete.** Elapsed: 49 min (18:44:15 → 19:33:30).

Parent findings: total 3 = fixed 3 + already fixed 0 + blocked 0 + deferred 0 + unresolved 0. Instance and consumer rows: 90 child rows, each fixed or clean (none open).

- **R4-F1** fixed: `written_path` builds the path by grammar shape. It covers roots (`::f`), right-nested C++ scopes, generic wrappers (`template_type`, `template_function`, `obj.template m`), C# alias-qualified and conditional access, PHP nullsafe access and namespace segments, Perl package and receiver forms, and Java/Scala/Swift/Go type paths. A written path makes a name uncertain before lexical lookup, so a rooted Rust name never binds a shadowing local, and a rooted self-call is `Unknown`, never `No`
- **R4-F2** fixed: rooted and C# conditional callees climb the same one step as paths. PHP callable creation (`variadic_placeholder`) is a Usage, while real unpacking still calls. PHP variable callees through any parentheses are calls, and the constant and property exceptions are kept. Perl code-reference invocations are calls, and their name-only forms stay usages
- **R3-F3** fixed: each stored occurrence carries a required, cache-only `path_start_byte`, which is `null` iff the path is `null`. `validate_evidence` refuses any disagreement between the two fields, or with the source, in all 16 languages, including omitted-member entries. This replaces the retained-member-only null rule. Deleting a path and its start together is a coordinated two-field forgery, and the review places it outside the required repair; `docs/cache-design.md` documents it. The nine missing robustness-matrix columns (R3-F3.M) are added
- the earlier S09r deferral is withdrawn: the docs no longer describe a lost qualifier as an accepted limit
- `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` 42 → 43 (one bump for the cycle); goldens regenerated (extractor version only)
- extra fixes beyond the review rows, all pinned by tests: Java/Scala `new a.b.C()` is now a `Call` of `C`, and Java/Scala/Swift/Go type paths and PHP namespace segments now keep their paths
- observed outside this feature, not changed: `hug refs` text shows the C# method header `g(W obj)` as `g(W: W)`, which looks like a signature/parameter display defect in declaration rendering. It should be triaged as a separate fix

- starting the work on 'R5-F4 — Parameter captures turn type and default-value references into declarations' at 20:06:28
        - defect class: a locals-query declaration pattern matched any identifier child of a binder node (a parameter, constant element, walrus, class-body assignment target, `if let` pattern) rather than its binding-name field, so a type, default value, constant value or pattern path became a declaration whose span then hid the real read; the only rejection layer (`drop_non_declarations`/`is_false_capture`) runs for Rust and TS/JS alone, so limited languages kept the fake records
        - repair: narrowed the queries to binding fields rather than widening the rejection layer (one place per site, no language table in Rust code); Python `default_parameter name:`, `typed_default_parameter name:`, `named_expression name:`, class-body targets only inside `pattern_list`/`tuple_pattern`/`list_pattern`; C# `parameter name:`; PHP `simple_parameter name:`, `property_element name:`, `(const_element . (name))`; Rust `let_condition` lists the pattern kinds whose identifier children bind
        - the C# `g(W: W)` display was a second reader with the same defect: `extract_csharp_parameter` took the first identifier child (the type) as the name and looked for an `equals_value_clause` the 0.23.5 grammar never produces; it now reads the `name`/`type` fields and the expression after `=` (`g(obj: W)`, default `external`)
        - sweep (all 16 locals queries, every `@local.definition.*` capture without a field or anchor): Python `parameters`/`typed_parameter`/splat children (binders only, clean), `default_parameter`/`typed_default_parameter`/`named_expression`/class-body `left: (_ …)` (fixed); C# `parameter` (fixed), `variable_declarator .`, `type_parameter`, `tuple_pattern` (clean), `using_directive` (alias and target both captured, but import captures make no records); PHP `simple_parameter`, `property_element`, `const_element` (fixed), `foreach_statement` two-sibling form, `pair`, `anonymous_function_use_clause` (clean); Rust `let_condition pattern: (_ …)` (fixed: range bounds, `E::A` path segments, generic-pattern paths, macro names), `tuple_pattern`, `closure_parameters` (clean), `tuple_struct_pattern` (path guarded by `is_false_capture`), `use_list` (imports, no records); TS `required_parameter`/`optional_parameter` (value guarded by `is_false_capture`, clean); JS/ecma `formal_parameters`, `array_pattern`, `rest_pattern`, `function_declaration` (clean), `import_specifier` (imports); Go `parameter_declaration`/`variadic_parameter_declaration` (types are never `identifier`, clean); Java `inferred_parameters` (clean), `scoped_identifier` imports (no records); C/C++ `attributed_declarator`, `parenthesized_declarator`, `reference_declarator`, `variadic_declarator`, `type_parameter_declaration` (declarator child only, clean); Lua `parameters`, `variable_list`, anchored `dot_index_expression` (clean); Scala/Swift `import_declaration` (imports); Perl, Bash, Zsh (field-precise, clean)
        - discoveries: the Python class-body pattern also needed `list_pattern` (`[e, f] = 1, 2`), caught by `symbols_field_lists::python_chained_and_pattern_assignments_are_listed`; Java `int... r` and Python lambda/annotated-variadic binders are not captured at all (unreported binders, R5-F2 territory, not touched)
        - verification: every defective row of `PARAMETER_CAPTURE_MATRIX` failed with the four queries reverted and passes now; control rows passed both ways
        - completed: `just test -j 4` in `tree-hugger/`: 1403 passed, 0 skipped; `just lint`: exit 0
- work completed for 'R5-F4 — Parameter captures turn type and default-value references into declarations' at 20:25:38

        - defect class: for the 13 languages without lexical rules, occurrence exclusion only knew the declarations that became records plus a short, mostly Rust/TS-oriented parent-kind fallback (`is_definition_like_reference`); there was no complete per-language inventory of the syntactic places a name is declared, so every unreported declaration token (generic parameters, pattern binders, labels, lambda parameters, destructuring targets, …) was captured by the references query and reported as a `Usage`

### Completion Report

**Outcome: complete.** Elapsed: 1 h 34 min (20:05:07 → 21:39:30).

Parent findings: total 4 = fixed 4 + already fixed 0 + blocked 0 + deferred 0 + unresolved 0. Instance, consumer and sweep rows: 182 child rows. Of these, 108 are fixed, 73 are clean (one is "clean (decided)" and one is n/a), and 1 is blocked by a grammar limit. One discovered block (R5-X) is outside the review's findings, and its two defective rows are fixed.

- **R5-F4** fixed: the Python, C#, PHP and Rust `if let` locals-query captures now bind only the binding-name fields, so types, defaults, constant values and pattern paths are references again. The C# signature reader now reads the `name`/`type` fields and the default expression, so `g(W obj)` now displays as `g(obj: W)` instead of `g(W: W)`. `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` 43 → 44 (once for the cycle), with a revision-43 stale-entry test
- **R5-F2** fixed: a new per-language declaration-name inventory, `analysis/declaration_names.rs`, excludes declaration tokens in the 13 limited languages before candidate selection, without adding records. It covers the 32 defective review rows plus sweep rows S1–S20; the S20 wildcard `_` was added by the orchestrator
- **R5-F1** fixed: `generic_operand` now covers Java/Scala `generic_type`, C++ `template_type` and Swift generic `user_type`. Swift `constructor_expression` and C++ braced construction are now classified as calls. `implied_operand` handles Swift implicit members and `!f()`, plus the Scala mixin base. Swift subscripts (S17) are no longer calls. C++ array `new T[n]` is now a Usage, the same as Java and C# array creation
- **R5-F3** fixed: a Java construction receiver is kept in the written path (`outer.new C`). Swift prefix operators are no longer absorbed into a path: `!x.isEmpty()` → `x.isEmpty`. Sweep siblings fixed: Python starred targets (`*x.s` → `x.s`, R5-X.06) and PHP static members (`X::K`, `X::$f`, R5-X.10)
- open row: **R5-F1.S12** is blocked. tree-sitter-swift 0.7.1 parses `C<Int>.init()` as comparisons. The kinds are correct (`init` Call, `C` Usage, matching `C.init()`), but `init` has no written path. The next action is a grammar upgrade; the limit is documented in `docs/references.md`
- observations outside the review's defect classes, not changed:
        - the experimental semantic lint's `TreeFile::referenced_symbol` still uses the older declaration fallback, not the new inventory. The occurrence contract does not depend on it
        - plain PHP `$x = …` assignment stays a Usage, because PHP has no declaration syntax and the review asked only for destructuring
        - Lua `<const>`/`<close>` attribute names and the Java `var` type token are still reported as occurrences
- no performance measurement was required; `deferred_perf_measurement` stays `false`

### Completion Report

**Outcome: complete.** Elapsed: 1 h 1 min (21:53:11 → 22:54:30).

Parent findings: total 3 = fixed 3 + already fixed 0 + blocked 0 + deferred 0 + unresolved 0. Child rows: 67 (63 from the review plus 4 discovered: `R6-F3.S1`, `.S2`, `.X1`, `.X2`). Of these, 45 are fixed, 21 clean, and 1 is split: `R6-F1.17` is fixed for its parenthesized spellings (.17a) and blocked by the pinned grammar for two spellings (.17b).

- **R6-F1** fixed: `wrapper_operand` treats Swift `postfix_expression` with a `bang` operation and C# `postfix_unary_expression` ending in exactly `!` as transparent wrappers, at any depth. `cb!()`, `(cb!)()`, `x.cb!()`, `obj.F!()`, `obj?.F!()`, `cb!!()` (C#) and their grouped forms are now Calls. Written paths, candidates, Limited coverage and Unknown recursion are unchanged. `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` 44 → 45, once for the cycle.
- **R6-F2** fixed: the C# parameter reader skips comments when finding `=` and the value after it. PHP and Scala read the grammar's `default_value` field. The shared adjacent-child helper is deleted, and the Swift single-parameter helper no longer reads a default the grammar never puts there. Sweep siblings fixed in the same change: C# `params /* c */ int[] xs` (the parameter was lost) and Swift `typealias A = /* c */ Int` (the comment was reported as the aliased type).
- **R6-F3** fixed: the new `analysis/syntax_markers.rs` excludes contextual markers by grammar role before candidate selection: Java `var` in inferred-type slots, Lua `<const>`/`<close>`, and the sweep siblings PHP `self`/`static`/`parent` (S1) and shell declaration-builtin options (S2). A file that declares or single-type imports a type named `var` is legacy source, and its `var` stays a usage.
- **Discovered and fixed:** X1, a Java bare-identifier initializer (`int k = xs;`) lost its read because `is_named_definition_node` listed `value` as a name field. As a side effect, the experimental `unused-symbol` rule stops falsely flagging such variables and TS/JS default-read variables. Severity, confidence and default enablement are unchanged. X2, Zsh declaration-builtin operands leaked as usages; they are now excluded, matching Bash.
- **Open child row:** `R6-F1.17b`, Swift `cb!!()` and `cb?!()`. tree-sitter-swift (lock 0.7.3) parses `!!`/`?!` as custom infix operators with a MISSING node, so `cb` stays a Usage in a `parse_error`-limited region. Next action: a grammar upgrade. The limit is documented in `docs/references.md` and pinned by `swift_adjacent_unwrap_operators_are_a_grammar_limit`. Like `R5-F1.S12`, this is a pinned-grammar limit under the accepted release boundary, not unimplemented work.
- `R5-F1.S12` (Swift `C<Int>.init()` path) remains a documented grammar limit and is unchanged this cycle.
- Observations, not review rows and not changed: PHP plain assignment targets (`$xs = 1`) remain usages (accepted in iteration 5). Ordinary-command operands such as `unset x` and `read x` remain usages.

### Progress

- starting the work on 'R7-F1 syntax markers' at 23:28:23
        - defect class: identifier-shaped syntax markers became references because a whole-file marker gate accepted a static member import as evidence of a legacy type, and because the marker inventory had no arm for a grammar role that writes compiler-configuration words as identifiers
        - discovery: `java_declared_type_name` never checked the `static` token, so `import static p.Tools.var;` (a field or method) switched the file into legacy mode and every inference `var` became a Usage/NameOnly; a static import is now never evidence (it names fields and methods as readily as a member type), while an ordinary single-type import, declared classes/interfaces/enums/annotation types/type parameters stay evidence; a `var` qualifier (`import p.var.T;`) and on-demand imports were already no evidence and stay so (rows .09)
        - discovery: tree-sitter-swift 0.7.1 writes `precedence_group_attribute` as `simple_identifier ':' (simple_identifier | boolean_literal)`; the directive is always a marker, the value only after `associativity`; `higherThan`/`lowerThan` values and an operator's `: P` group stay reads
        - discovery: the Swift reference query captures every `simple_identifier`, so four more compiler-configuration roles leaked the same way (new sibling rows R7-F1.S1-.S4): a pre-Swift 3 `deprecated_operator_declaration_body`, `availability_condition` platforms (`#available(iOS 13, *)`), `@available(...)` arguments, and `directive` (`#if os(macOS) && DEBUG`) operands; all fixed by role in one `swift_markers` arm; other attributes' arguments (`@W(message)`) stay reads
        - discovery (not fixed, reported for a decision): named-argument labels are captured as Usage/NameOnly in Swift (`f(ext: 1)`, tuple labels), Python (`f(x=x)`), C# (`f(x: y)`), PHP (`f(x: 1)`) and Scala (`f(x = x)`); a label names the callee's parameter rather than reading a binding, a cross-language rule outside the marker inventory (R7-F1.S5); Swift attribute names (`@available`, `@objc`) stay usages because property wrappers, result builders and global actors are real types in that slot, and `Q.Type` parses as a `user_type` segment rather than `metatype` (R7-F1.S6, grammar limit)
        - sites swept: every marker arm (Java inference slots and legacy gate, Lua attribute, PHP class operand, Bash/Zsh declaration options), the new Swift arm, and keyword roles in rs, ts, js, py, go, c, cpp, cs, scala and perl by an independent CLI probe: no keyword or modifier token is captured in those languages; `any`/`comparable`, C# `dynamic`/`nameof` and Python soft-keyword names remain reads
        - consumers: exclusion happens in `Walk::markers` before `Resolver::excluded_names`, so no marker reaches NameOnly lookup; the Java lambda keeps its anonymous region (`marker_exclusions_keep_legacy_reads_group_references_and_anonymous_regions`); limited languages keep Limited coverage, no edges and Unknown recursion (`assert_exclusion_rows`); six cache stages and cold/warm CLI default reports covered

        - decision per role: nothing in `export … from "m"` is an occurrence (as for an `import` statement); an `export_specifier`'s `alias` and the `export as namespace` name are no occurrence; a local export's own name (`export { k }`, `export { k as j }`'s `k`, `export = k`) stays a `Scoped` read; `export import A = N.S` binds `A` (import record) and reads `N`; export records and `is_reexported` untouched
        - fix: `occurrences::is_ecma_export_name`, applied in the occurrence filter and in `TreeFile::referenced_symbol` (diagnostics references); summaries follow from the occurrences (no edge came from ownerless file-level export tokens, `R7-F3.X2.06` pins `g` → `k` only and no dependencies)
        - Go raw-string paths (fixed): the query captures `raw_string_literal` paths too and `go_import_path` strips either quote, so `` import `c/d` `` → `d` source `c/d` and `` import x `e/f` `` → `x` original `f` source `e/f` (before: absent, and `f\``/`\`e/f\``); escapes in interpreted paths are not decoded (not exercised)
        - tests: `symbols_occurrences::EXPORT_CLAUSE_MATRIX` rows R7-F3.X2.01-.06 (exact whole-file occurrences, Scoped targets by record, summaries, diagnostics references), `GO_IMPORT_MATRIX` R7-F3.X3.01, `tree_file::go_raw_string_import_paths_match_interpreted_ones`, cache sources exports.ts and r.go in `qualified_reads_and_imports_hold_through_the_cache`, CLI `refs::export_clause_names_are_never_references` (ID-selected `k` and `j`, TS and JS, JSON cold miss/warm hit and text) and `refs::go_raw_string_imports_are_listed_by_imports`

### Completion Report

**Outcome: complete.** Elapsed: 2 h 50 min (23:26:59 → 02:16:30).

Parent findings: total 3 = fixed 3 + already fixed 0 + blocked 0 + deferred 0 + unresolved 0. Child rows: 109 (82 from the review plus 27 discovered: R7-F1.S1–.S6, R7-F2.S1–.S8, R7-F2.X1, R7-F3.S1–.S7, R7-F3.X1–.X3, and two non-finding observations). Of the 107 non-observation rows, 96 are fixed and 11 clean. The two observations (R7-OBS.1, .2) are deferred, outside this spec's occurrence contract.

- **R7-F1** fixed: a static import no longer counts as evidence of a legacy type named `var`. Swift precedence directives and associativity values are excluded by grammar role. The sweep also excluded Swift `#available`/`@available`/`#if`/legacy-operator markers, named-argument and tuple labels in seven languages, and Swift built-in attribute names (`@objc`) by a list pinned to Swift 5.10.
- **R7-F2** fixed: one shared `declared_name_tokens` excludes every segment of a declared package, module or namespace path and the names of annotation types, static constructors, accessor events, extern aliases, Swift macros, precedence groups and selective imports. The TypeScript namespace prefix no longer binds to a same-named class or creates fabricated summary edges. The grammar sweep fixed eight more gaps (S1–S8). C/C++ elaborated type uses no longer become Type records (X1).
- **R7-F3** fixed: the Java import query is import-scoped by structure instead of an unenforced `#has-ancestor?` predicate. A new guard test fails if any query uses a predicate the runtime does not enforce. Qualified annotations and every module-directive operand are reads again, including through `referenced_symbols`. The import-reader sweep fixed binding records in C#, PHP, Python, Rust, Scala, Swift, TS/JS and Go, plus TS/JS export-clause names and Go raw-string paths.
- `EXTRACTOR_REVISION` 45 → 46 once for the cycle (`cache_upgrade::entry_from_extractor_revision_forty_five_…`).
- Carried grammar limits `R6-F1.17b` and `R5-F1.S12` are unchanged and remain nonblocking under the accepted release boundary, as the review states.
- Deferred observations: R7-OBS.1 (relation-tree resolver lacks a Python `__future__` arm; the next action is to add it with a relation test when that resolver is next touched); R7-OBS.2 (`hug imports` plain-text display shapes; records/JSON are correct).
- No performance measurement was required; `deferred_perf_measurement` stays `false`.
